Have had periods in life when I was immuno-depressed and caught every bug going. Even when I had got better and stronger,  exposure to students and colleagues ( some of whom were obviously coughing and sneezing in the winter months) meant 1-2 bad episodes each winter.
Staying at home/working from home/online lessons and exams/little or no socialization during Covid  meant I was better than ever – no colds, no flus etc . Lack of exposure also strengthened me going forward.
Moral of the story – try to avoid people in enclosed environments.Open windows for as long as possible each day at home, in a meeting/lecture hall.Anywhere you are spending a lot of time. .Eat and drink outside with friends if you can – in their gardens, outdoor restaurants, beer gardens,pavement cafes etc,

I was still skeptical it wasn’t COVID with all the denialism when I saw this earlier this week, but I know Yves takes COVID seriously.
So that’s very disturbing.
I have had 2 patients in the past month that have been sick since July.  Severe pulmonary and nasal congestion, intermittent fever, shortness of breath, constant productive coughing and just disabling malaise.  This scenario is quite frankly the ONLY time I ever use the comprehensive respiratory panels.  This is a test that identifies any pathogens from a list of dozens.  This includes all the viral pathogens – not just the common ones like flu and COVID – but ECHO, EBV, Parainflenza, etc among many many others.  Their tests have been completely negative.  The main reason I do not order these tests commonly are two fold – a) it really does not matter which virus is involved the vast majority of the time in short term illness – the treatment is exactly the same and b) this test is insanely expensive and again the vast majority of the time in normal illness is uniquely unhelpful for all the expense.  But when this type of thing is going on and illnesses are lasting months, I feel obligated to be thorough – not just for the patient in front of me – but also for the public health department.  
Just FYI – there is a similar study for GI pathogens and diarrhea.  It will cost the patient or their insurance company about 3K.  
I was trained in an environment where tests were only ordered if there was some very good reason AND we were going to act upon them.  I will share with everyone that the NPs and PAs in urgent cares around the country order these viral studies literally all the time on every single patient with the slightest sniffle or the slightest diarrhea.  I know this because I read my patient’s medical lectures – and sit through many patient lectures about being charged 5K for an urgent care visit and how insane is our health system.  I nod my head and tell them I agree.  Despite the NPs and PAs doing this over and over again with the same unhelpful results, they seem to be blissfully unaware of the enormous cost – and the times I have talked to them about it they become immediately and openly hostile.   I have given up even trying.  The train will very soon hit the brick wall.
However, as worthless as doing these tests for a patient with 3 days of sniffles, it is really quite important for this to be done in patients like this tweet is describing.  I would say that over the past few years, I am seeing way more of these extended and unexplained illnesses than I ever have before.  I have many thoughts – but no firm evidence of what is going on.

I have some doubts about the video showing the wolf howling — not the wolf itself, but the “forest answer”. 
It takes place in winter. I find it strange that active animals would be so plentiful. In Spring or Summer I would not have been surprised by a multitude of calls coming from a forest. 
The sounds themselves are a bit odd. Is this a chicken cackling near by at the 30 seconds mark? And other birds later on? Was the video taken in a forest, or in an place such as a zoological park?
The pups of the summer turn juveniles in the late autumn or early winter, and when they learn to hunt, they and the leading male communicate by howling. The very thin snow layer and the fact that it’s daytime indicate that this could be the case in the video.
Anecdotally, the coyotes here are vocal this time of year. I have read that, through interbreeding they have about 40% eastern grey wolf dna. They certainly look like wolves. I’ve seen three up close, all easily fifty pound dogs.

‘Union Town’ Review: Barbara Kopple’s Labor Rights Documentary Is Both Aptly Angry and Optimistically Community-Minded
Completing a workers’ rights-themed trilogy after the Oscar-winning ‘Harlan County U.S.A.’ and ‘American Dream,” Kopple portrays the struggles of New York City delivery workers with clear-eyed purpose.
https://variety.com/2026/film/reviews/union-town-review-1236854414/
“(…)
Across the harrowing financial realities of late capitalism, an indisputable global fact is the injustices faced by countless workers who deliver everything we have come to rely on, from food orders to Amazon purchases. Despite their hard work under unideal, sometimes even unsafe conditions, it’s no secret that delivery staff have been barely compensated with dignified wages, a topic that Oscar-winning filmmaker Barbara Kopple tackles in her fiery documentary “Union Town.” Focused on a variety of recent legal cases unfolding in New York, Kopple’s film is both aptly angry and optimistically community-minded in charting the fight of several Big Apple workforce groups: delivery staff, union leaders, Teamsters and others trying to bring about meaningful change to an increasingly corrupt system that leaves these hourly workers behind.

It is not “this business”.  Thailand is deadly serious.
The Thai government is months into a massive, ongoing crackdown on three overlapping matters:
1. Illegal nominee structures (with only limited and specific exceptions, only Thais can be majority owners of businesses).  Thais are enlisted to act as straws, putting up no capital and playing no role in company operations. These have been used for money laundering, to evade Thai taxes by running “zero dollar” operations, and #2 below
2. Illegal ownership of land. Only Thais can own land, again with limited exceptions.
3. Illegal acquisition of Thai citizenship, to use in #1 and #2.
All sorts of raids have been done with Chinese, Russians, Indians, French, Australian etc nationals being arrested and likely to have property stripped from them. But the Israelis are offenders out of proportion to their numbers as tourists and long-term visa holders. 
Specifically, re Rabbi Kantor, he will be stripped of any property or land where his Thai citizenship was integral to the holding (as in he was a >50% owner). Moreover, Thailand does not recognize birthright citizenship. Children who had been born while he was a citizen would have been Thai citizens. However, the noise about the investigation suggests that they will be stripped of it too, either directly or by virtue of traveling using a non-Thai passport, and have any now-illegal property seized.

I am not sure when lobotomy treatments were halted. A WIKI reference [https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lobotomy] indicated their use continued into the 1950s and remained in practice until the 1970s. I’ve read both your comments, Christian B., and I hear your frustration and concern.  I’m especially interested in your comment about catecholamines.  Two years ago, a tumor in my right adrenal turned out to be a pheochromocytoma rather than a metastasis from my rectal cancer.  That was determined by checking the normetanephrines and catecholamines, both of which were very elevated.  (This is GOOD news, the doctor said.)  My right adrenal is now gone, and the normetanephrine level is now normal, but the catecholamines have come down, but are still above normal, perhaps because of a bone spot now thought to be related to the pheochromo.
I know that catecholamines raise blood pressure and blood sugar, which is their proper role in a fight-or-flight situation.  In fact, getting an adrenal gland removed is quite tricky because the blood pressure jumps during the surgery (mine hit 233).  What is the effect on mental health?
Catecholamines include dopamine, norepinephrine, and epinephrine. So higher leveles might be linked to psychosis, anxiety, or labile hypertension. Basically bipolar mania. This all depends of course on other genetics and environment. Basically any amine will affect mood, which also inclused serotonin and the trace amines like the amine in chocolate called PEA. You might find this paper on Sci-Hub: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/0959437X94900310?via%3Dihub
I would say it is good that your Catecholamines are high/normal after getting that tumor removed. Just to be clear, Normetanephrine is a metabolite of norepinephrine, and is the only way to test norepinephrine levels, but is is not an exact match. 
233 blood pressure? Ha, that is nothing. Each time I was hospitalized my BP was at least 270/120. Did they care? Nope. 
I know, like me, that you are a very spiritual person, and  IMHO, this is probably what we share. Since my hypertension is more reactive they ruled out a pheochromocytoma for me. My issue is too much ATP and GTP, amoung other genetic freakishness but that is a long story.

Excerpt:
Blackstone told investors Anthropic may not receive an investment-grade rating immediately after going public, a reversal from its previous guidance that would complicate the AI lab’s ability to finance its massive spending.
The private capital giant told investors in Anthropic’s latest $63bn chip financing deal that the start-up is expected to receive a “high” junk rating from major credit rating agencies after its initial public offering, according to people familiar with the matter. […]
While Blackstone had told its investment partners in recent months that it was expecting an investment-grade rating for Anthropic before year-end, it has recently signalled that a rating in the double-B range or above is more likely given the guidance it has received from major credit agencies, the people said.
Such a designation would limit the pool of capital that Anthropic could tap because many insurance and pension funds are restricted from taking large positions in junk-rated borrowers. As a result, the company might need to continue to rely on its suppliers with high credit ratings to support its debt-raising efforts. […]
“THE LEGLESS PODCASTERS — WHY THE NAPOLITANO GANG MAKES THE SAME WAR MISTAKE SO OFTEN”
I’m not sure if Helmer has a bone to pick with Napolitano specifically or if this piece reflects a broader discomfort with the constant-attention-economy business model that is his podcasting being applied, now, to journalism. Regardless, it’s an interesting article with some analysis of the figures on the one hand, and some fairly common sense statements about the perverse incentives of running a news and analysis outlet on youtube.
Besides that, I’m not convinced – and I’m not certain Helmer is trying to convince us, for all his sarcasm and jabbing – that Napolitano is a bad actor in the alt media content machine really. For the last few years I’ve thought of his show as a gateway drug, for the uninitiated, to more thorough and reflective content on the same issues.
Yes. I think Helmer definitely has a bone to pick with Putin, so this is probably behind a lot of his sarcasm toward those who “condone and commend” his policies. A lot of his descriptive comments are accurate, as you point out. Yet I do not think his “carnival barker” insinuations are fair. It is true that the Judge’s guests, especially two of his most popular (Ritter and MacGregor), have tended to be overly optimistic in their pro-Russian predictions and one-sided in their analyses.  But in agreement with your last point, they serve a useful purpose in countering the even more misleading “factoids, disinformation, wishful thinking, [and] propaganda” that saturates the mainstream media in the US.  And I think Helmer is simply wrong when he says their emphasis on “drama” and Russian victories is a means of obfuscating
“… what the US is about in general, and up to in particular in the Ukraine. Better to show the Gadsden flag, eagles, Stars and Stripes, ‘Founding Father’ slogans, and a rotation of old men pining for the Russo-American rapprochement that is ‘the Great White Hope’.” 
I’m not fond of the Judge’s “Gadsden flag” and “eagles” opening; I often skip over it when watching clips. But the greatest value of the podcast is precisely to explain to viewers “what the US is about” and in particular what it has been doing in Ukraine – information they will get from few other sources. And if the Judge’s libertarian credentials and patriotic memes bring a somewhat broader audience to a Max Blumenthal interview, then I see that as a positive. The “Great White Hope” charge is just a smear comment that echoes the liberal “Putin = Trump = Orban = racist authoritarian” narrative.  Has Helmer watched any of the episodes on Israel or the Iran war?
Patriotic-nationalist-libertarian aesthetics and seldom-explicit (except when say, MacGregor is on) right wing preferences aside – you’re exactly right. Anyone exposed to Max Blumenthal is improved for it – in fact, Napolitano himself may have so been.
I’ve never quite understood Helmer’s politics or preferences or whatever you like. Sometimes I think he’s just an obligate contrarian and when he sees too much of a mass-zigging phenomenon he zags much sooner than anyone else. It’s useful to have people like that and he’s a dextrous writer so generally pleasant to read, but his worldview as a whole still doesn’t make sense to me, to the extent he has one.

Re car “head units”–I looked to see if Amazon had one for my 2017 car and surprisingly (to me) they did and under $200 so not that expensive. Specs don’t list a cell radio and one reason to replace the factory original might be to get rid of this. As for junk Android apps, the unit would have to be connected to the web via wifi (which it has) for any of these to spy on you. Without a cell radio the replacement might mean less spying unless there is a secret radio broadcasting to Beijing. In theory the car’s original radio doesn’t use cell either unless you subscribe to a service.
I believe some models actually have a second cell modem in the Telematics Control Unit (TCU), that can also conduct surveillance.
E.g., one of my friends discovered that after he returned a leased car, the dealer didn’t reset the TCU or the access password, and he was able to retrieve GPS info on the car’s location from the vendor’s cloud, even months after it had been leased to somebody else. 
His comment: “this is how people can be blackmailed.”

You haven’t run across of Crutchfield, I see.
Just because the “head unit” is behind a monolithic piece of plastic, doesn’t mean it isn’t there. DIN and Double-DIN are indeed antiquated standards for “head units,” IMO because it was to the advantage of the auto-makers to make it more difficult for citizens to avoid the mandatory initial purchase audio upgrade costs imposed by the new non-standards.
Like headlights – I became an automobile pilot when there were three (maybe four?) headlights: two sizes of round and one size of rectangular.
The Chinese have figured out how to get around the non-standards, in many cases.
I replaced the factory speakers with some Pioneers from Crutchfield shortly after I bought the Japanese car. Just went to the site and did find aftermarket head units or stereos in several form factors,  and it looks like it would take more work choosing the right one (or combination) than I’m willing to invest, to say nothing of the actual labor or required trim tools.
FWIW I designed VCMs for Chrysler and John Deere vehicles in the late 90s at Motorola so I’m familiar with what is involved replacing auto electronics, but I’m lazy and most of the year weather is unbearable here.

I have done that several times and a few different types of vehicles. Easiest was small to mid size trucks for model years 2012 to 2020. It wasn’t too bad in a Subaru forester either. Good reasons to do it are upgrading to Bluetooth connections, better functionality if you want to use apps while in your vehicle. Other times the native set up is just bad. Subaru’s systems are grabby and for a lot of model years just start playing music regardless of what you’ve told it to do when connected.
As for connectivity and tracking, depending on the apps you use, they may be doing it already. NC has hosted articles describing how services like Spotify track how many times you interact with it in a vehicle while it is moving and then report it. Insurance companies then take that data and give you a score. It probably doesn’t matter if the radio unit has independent cell service if you need an app on your phone to interact with the device.

A pleasant surprise to see some metal representation here at NC! Plenty of great picks on the list too, although I can kinda tell it was written by someone who’s maybe a little too close to the genre. Mayhem’s DMDS is a certified classic, but I think Attila’s vocal style probably comes off a little too goofy to outsiders to really make it a top-spot inclusion. I think also its impact is heavily dependent on how familiar one is with the abundant drama of the Norwegian second-wave scene. Genre tourists would benefit greatly from learning a bit about the sordid history of that milieu.
For my money, the French selections (Deathspell & Blut Aus Nord) are the finest on offer here, the latter of which is possessed by an overbearing lifeless dread that’s quite unique. Deathspell’s SMRC, while a great album in its own right is something of a debut for them, and the least-mature work of their mature phase – it only gets better from there on out, and they are among a very tiny contingent of still-vital forces in the extreme metal sphere. Like most other musical subcultures, it ran out of ideas decades ago and has degraded into self-reflexive worship and genre-mashups. There are talented, enthusiastic young bands putting out competent material of course, but they’re all just rehashing ideas that have already been exhausted.

Obamacare Dealt Yet Another Blow as Premiums Rise Again (NY Times; archive)
Millions of Americans who depend on Obamacare for their health insurance face another double-digit price increase next year, adding to the financial toll that has already driven millions of others to drop coverage since last year.
The price increases largely reflect higher medical costs across the country. But they also are a signal of the latest blow to Obamacare under the Trump administration, which has overseen the expiration of tax credits that helped Americans pay for coverage and has erected new barriers to enrollment in an effort to root out what it describes as widespread fraud.
The average price of health insurance in the Obamacare marketplaces will rise by 13 percent next year, according to an analysis on Friday by KFF, the health research group.

The warning, buried in an obscure government report this summer, was unflinching.
The Defense Department’s vaunted network of research laboratories — whose scientists helped develop satellite communication, radar technology, and even the computer mouse — was in trouble.
The labs lost a significant share of their staff last year, largely in response to the Trump administration’s efforts to thin the federal work force, the report found. Refilling critical positions was proving difficult due in part to President Trump’s demand that new hires be screened for their loyalty to his goals. The report, issued by the Pentagon, pointed to “potentially grave consequences for the future of national defense.”

For those who may be interested, at the time of writing (17h45 CET) the demonstrations in France have finished and the protesters have dispersed. The demonstrations were peaceful and good-humoured. Turnout was substantial, but continues to decline slightly.
What is now happening is that groups of the demonstrators, and new arrivals in the traditional black clothing with masks and helmets, are confronting the police. So far, it’s the traditional stone-throwing mixed with home-made explosives, to which the Police reply with CS gas. Most of the town-centres are deserted and boarded up, but the Police are trying to stop rioters from getting into areas where they could do damage. Some shops seem to have been set on fire, but so far things have not got out of hand. However, it’s getting dark, and there’s a general expectation that things will get nastier. There are two special factors as well. One is that it’s Halloween (imported from the US), which has quickly become an occasion for random violence in certain areas, like the ritual burning of cars at New Year. The other is that extreme-Right groups have been active on WhatsApp, apparently trying to organise counter-violence against the rioters. However, such groups hate the police as much as the others do, if it happens, it’ll be a three-cornered fight. 
Just bear in mind that anything reported as happening after about 18h00 CET today will not be part of the “demonstrations,” but something quite separate.
Thanks for the update.  If the newcomers turn out to be troublemakers then the MSM will no doubt try to tar the peaceful demonstrators as the troublemakers.  In the US we saw that happen during the BLM protests.

Two supertankers carrying over 2 million barrels of oil and another 3 vessels were attacked yesterday while trying to avoid the Iranian Navy designated maritime route.
Why in the world don’t ship operators just cooperate with Iran and pay the toll allowing their ships to pass through the Straight of Hormuz without Iranian attacks?  It can’t be a matter of the money itself.  I assume that the Iranian government would happily go along with the arrangement, since they proposed it and set up the infrastructure for a toll. 
Businesses pay protection money (and taxes and insurance premiums!) all the time for the sake of having a smoothly operating business. Even if you are wildly opposed to what Iran is demanding, every business person should understand it and know how to coexist with it.
What am I missing?
From 6 months ago:
The US Treasury warns any shipping company that pays Iran tolls to transit Hormuz could face sanctions
https://www.msn.com/en-us/news/world/the-us-treasury-warns-any-shipping-company-that-pays-iran-tolls-to-transit-hormuz-could-face-sanctions/ar-AA22jbwx
Caught between a rock and a hard place, as they say.
https://regulasshipping.com/blog/insurers-move-to-restrict-cover-for-ships-paying-iran-tolls-through-the-strait-of-hormuz/
“The clarification comes from a major insurance trade body and effectively puts owners on notice: if a vessel’s transit involves payment of a toll or fee to Iran to secure safe passage, the underlying insurance policy could be voided. This is a meaningful shift because informal toll or fee arrangements have, at various points during regional conflicts, been floated or used as a practical way for shipping companies to reduce transit risk. That workaround now carries a direct insurance consequence.”
This is from July.  
Iran tried this:
https://www.agbi.com/analysis/shipping/2026/09/iranian-insurance-coercion-raises-shipping-sanctions-risks/

95,2026-10-31,"by Michael Levanduski
Shutterstock
Some people are very entitled and seem to think that they deserve to have everything handed to them on a platter.
That was the case in this story, where a guy came into a hotel demanding free water for himself and his friends because he had reached gold elite status.
The worker, unfortunately, had to tell him no since gold elite status doesn’t even earn one bottle of water (that is platinum), much less unlimited free water. Of course, the customer was very upset and threatened to call corporate.
It must be hard not to laugh at people like this. Read through the full story to see for yourself.
Working at a Hotel has been a mainly positive experience for me. But every once in a while you get those guests.
These loyalty programs are pretty standard.
The ones that seem to feel like it’s their world and we are the measly scum beneath their feet that’s so purpose is to hold them up.
Our Hotel has a reward system where after a certain amount of nights, you receive different statuses (Silver, Gold, and Platinum). Once you receive Platinum, you instantly get a gift upon checking in.
It could be an honest mistake.
This gift can vary from hotel to hotel but usually they are all the same. Either points to their rewards number, or a free item from the Market.
The person in question was a GOLD. Meaning that he was not yet even eligible for a free market item. He strolls down to the Front Desk and grabs a water from the Market, and then starts walking away.
He has not introduced himself to me nor has he acknowledged my existence, so I have no idea as to why this man is taking a water right in front of me without paying..
He thinks he is higher status than he is.
“Um.. excuse me sir, I can ring you up here”
He stops in his tracks and gives me a “what the heck” Expression.
“I’m a Gold Elite, MY water is free”
Keeping the customer happy is always smart.
“Oh, I’m sorry their must be some confusion, your benefits as a rewards member entail you to a complimentary water upon check in when you reach Platinum status, but hey, don’t worry about the water this time, just thought I’d let you know so your not caught off guard at other Hotels.”
Thats right, I didn’t want to cause a scene, so I politely informed him the policy and gave him the water anyway. After all, usually it’s just a misunderstanding.
Of course, he has to pay for them.
10 minutes later he comes down with a group of about 7 and comes to the front desk, he grabs a water for each of them looks, then at me with a raised eyebrow and asks
“Do you want me to pay for these?”
He is not happy.
Uhh.. duh? I try to make a joke of it though.
“Unfortunately yes, it be nice if everything was free wouldn’t it?”
“Thats ridiculous, I always get my waters free!”
This should be obvious.
“I..”
“Every hotel I have ever stayed at has given me free water! You trying to tell me I have to pay as a Gold Elite for these!?”
…
“Well, when a rewards member is entitled to a free item, it is limited to one, it’s the same for Platinums as well I’m afraid.”
Wow, talk about entitled.
“Well they’ve never charged me before!”
“I cant speak for other brand hotels, but unfortunately here they are limited to one per Platinum, and unfortunately as a Gold your status technically doesn’t include it yet. But I was willing to waive it this time due to it being a misunderstanding.”
“Well waive these then!”
Is he going to email corporate about someone following policy?
“I’m afraid I can’t do that sir.”
The man mutters some obscenities beneath his breath and pulls out enough money for the waters. I silently ring them up and hand him the change.
“I will be emailing your corporate about this! I have never been treated so disrespectfully.”
What a jerk.
Sign up to get our BEST stories of the week straight to your inbox.
“I’m sorry I couldn’t be more helpful sir, but it is our policy, and I don’t make the rules.”
“That a Bull**** answer, and you know it”
There is no way this guy will actually email corporate.
He storms out with his group, who seem just as confused as I was.
I hope he wrote corporate though, cause they will be more then willing to verify what I said to him.
There is certainly no shortage of entitled jerks in the world, and this is just one of them.
The worker handled the situation perfectly, though. Read through the comments to see what other people had to say.
Don’t give him any ideas.

That isn’t much of a loyalty program.

He must have a very easy life.

Unfortunately, this is likely true.

If you enjoyed this story, check out this post about a man who finds his parking spot at a hocket game in jeopardy from a rude driver.
What does he think will happen?

He got free water, and he still wasn’t happy. You give some people an inch, and they try to take a mile.

Senior Contributing Editor, Workplace & Community
Michael Levanduski is a veteran journalist and digital culture specialist with over 15 years of professional writing and research experience. While his background spans emerging technology and complex technical subjects, Michael specializes in analyzing workplace ethics, property disputes, and viral interpersonal dynamics across modern digital forums.
Since joining TwistedSifter in 2024, Michael has authored some of the publication’s most widely read coverage on corporate policy, malicious compliance, and neighborhood relations. Rather than simply aggregating online debates, he brings rigorous research, organizational context, and an empathetic editorial voice to everyday human dilemmas—transforming raw forum discussions into insightful, highly engaging narratives.
When he isn’t covering internet culture, Michael enjoys traveling, keeping up with tech trends, and spending time with his family. Connect with Michael on LinkedIn and Facebook.

96,2026-10-31,"Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality
Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.
TL;DR: The service extends a prior 24/5 equity-data product into weekends and holidays. An oracle feed is not a guarantee of a redeemable cash-market price, and an off-hours derivative reference can diverge from the next opening auction.
Chainlink introduced its 24/7 data service on October 29 to supply reference prices for equities, commodities and other real-world assets beyond conventional exchange hours. It identified lending protocol Aave as the launch partner for using the feeds to value eligible collateral.
The most concrete starting point is the dated disclosure from Chainlink. It offers a specific basis for assessing Chainlink’s round-the-clock real-world asset price feeds without turning the story into unsupported price speculation.
The service extends a prior 24/5 equity-data product into weekends and holidays. Chainlink said off-hours references combine information from several perpetual-futures venues, with stale data filtering and outlier controls, rather than treating a closed stock exchange as if it were still publishing live prices.
Announcements in crypto often arrive before the data needed to price their longer-term significance. A sensible reading separates what has already been delivered from what still needs commercial adoption, technical validation or regulatory clearance.
The question for market participants is what Chainlink’s round-the-clock real-world asset price feeds establishes today and what remains uncertain. An immediate reaction is not the same as evidence of a durable economic outcome.
Continuous reference data can support longer trading sessions, lending and prediction markets. The larger question is whether financial risk engines can make defensible decisions on Saturday using prices derived from instruments whose liquidity and economics differ from the underlying asset.
A pilot, framework or technical partnership should not be conflated with a live secondary market. Investors need to know whether assets have actually been issued, whether redemption is possible and where responsibility sits if records disagree.
A durable signal would be consistent follow-through: actual usage, transparent reporting and a credible explanation of risk. Short-term token-price moves can be driven by macroeconomic conditions or leverage and may say little about whether the product or initiative succeeds over months and years.
None of this removes the need for independent assessment. The published evidence identifies a development, while its risk and long-term economics are still open to examination.
A tokenized share can trade or support a loan when the underlying cash market is closed, creating a mismatch between token liquidity and reference pricing. Oracles deliver data to smart contracts, but the usefulness of a quote depends on its source quality, freshness and suitability for liquidation decisions.
An oracle feed is not a guarantee of a redeemable cash-market price, and an off-hours derivative reference can diverge from the next opening auction. Protocols need conservative collateral factors, circuit breakers and governance around unusual market conditions.
Representing an asset on a blockchain is the relatively straightforward part of tokenization. Making that representation useful requires legal rights, reliable valuation, restrictions on eligible holders, custody and a clear process for settlement and corporate actions.
The immediate checklist is relatively straightforward: look for verifiable implementation details and avoid interpreting a stated target as an outcome that has already occurred.
Watch which assets gain coverage, how Aave manages liquidations outside exchange hours, and whether data stays robust during thin liquidity. Sustainable usage is more important than a claim that a particular market is now technically available every hour.
From a market perspective, the first task is to separate the scale of the announcement from the scale of economic activity that has actually occurred. A company may report a large addressable market, an institutional name or a future capability, but those are not equivalent to settled transactions or recurring fee income. This is why the primary disclosure is a better starting point than a trading narrative attached to it.
Investors also need to separate changes in infrastructure from changes in token demand. Revenue may accrue to a company, a protocol treasury or a service provider without necessarily creating a one-for-one benefit for holders of a related token. Market impact depends on adoption, competition and the design of whatever fees or incentives are involved.
Primary source: Chainlink’s primary announcement. This reporting reflects information available on October 31, 2026; targets, estimates and statements about future plans are attributed to their issuers.
